宏观经济点评 2026 年 11 月 03 日

10 月 PMI 数据点评：节前需求释放后回调，增量
订单仍待接续

[Table_Author] 
 魏争 分析师  沈夏宜 分析师

Email:weizheng@lczq.com Email:shenxiayi@lczq.com  
证书:S1320524100001 证书:S1320523020004

### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
购进价格指数下降 4.0 个百分点至 56.8%，出厂价格指数下降 2.3 个百分
点至 51.7%，均保持在扩张区间，指向工业品价格仍在上涨，上涨的扩散
程度较 9 月减弱。两项指数之差由 6.8 个百分点收窄至 5.1 个百分点，表
明购进端相对出厂端的价格上涨压力边际缓和。国家统计局指出，受国际
原油价格持续攀升影响，石油煤炭及其他燃料加工、化学纤维及橡胶塑料
制品等行业的购进和出厂价格指数均继续处于 60%以上高位1。在订单走
弱的背景下，高位能源价格仍可能强化部分企业控制采购、谨慎排产的倾
向。 
中小企业和消费品行业景气回调，装备订单仍有局部支撑。从企业规模
看，大型企业仍保持扩张，中小企业则回吐了 9 月的改善。10 月，大、
中、小型企业 PMI 分别下降 0.4、1.3 和 1.4 个百分点至 50.2%、48.4%
和 47.5%。大型与小型企业景气差距由 1.7 个百分点扩大至 2.7 个百分
点。从行业看，高技术制造业和装备制造业 PMI 分别下降 1.2 和 0.4 个百
分点至 51.3%和 50.6%，仍处于扩张区间，其中，装备制造业新订单指数
较上月上升 1 个百分点至 51%以上，或与“六张网”逐步推进形成实物工
作量有关；消费品行业 PMI 下降 1.9 个百分点至 48.8%，高耗能行业下降
0.7 个百分点至 47.3%。 
服务业景气小幅回落，假日消费形成支撑，新增需求仍显不足。 10 月，
服务业商务活动指数下降 0.2 个百分点至 50.0%，回到荣枯线。细分看，
一是行业表现分化，假日消费与信息服务仍有支撑。铁路运输、住宿、文
化体育娱乐等行业商务活动指数明显回升，邮政、互联网软件及信息技术
服务等行业保持在 55%以上；金融行业景气回落较大，是服务业总指数下
降的主要拖累。二是新增订单与销售价格同步转弱。新订单指数下降 1.5
个百分点至 45.2%，销售价格指数下降 1.0 个百分点至 49.6%，重新低于
荣枯线，指向新增需求承接及企业定价能力仍然偏弱。 三是用工景气边际
改善，但经营预期略有回落。从业人员指数上升 0.5 个百分点至 46.7%，
仍处于收缩区间；业务活动预期指数下降 0.3 个百分点至 55.2%，指向企
业对后续经营仍保持一定信心。 
建筑业景气重新回落，新增订单明显走弱，施工需求仍待释放。 10 月，
建筑业商务活动指数下降 2.3 个百分点至 48.0%，重返收缩区间。一是施
工活动放缓，内部表现分化。房屋建筑业商务活动指数明显回落至 50%以
下，土木工程建筑业指数回升，但仍低于荣枯线，指向部分基建施工边际
改善。二是新增订单明显收缩，后续施工需求承压。新订单指数下降 5.7
个百分点至 40.0%，显示新增项目需求景气进一步走弱，当前施工放缓 可
能并不只是既有项目建设节奏较慢的影响。三是用工与经营预期同步回
落。从业人员指数下降 2.3 个百分点至 42.0%，业务活动预期指数下降
3.1 个百分点至 52.3%，企业用工需求偏弱，对后续经营的预期也趋于谨
慎。 
短期回调能否修复，取决于新增订单能否接续。 10 月 PMI 边际下滑主因
节前集中生产和备货结束后，生产、订单、采购同步回调， 同时优势行业

### Page 3

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 3 
的局部扩张不足以带动制造业整体保持景气。往后看，一是需要观察新订
单与在手订单能否同步回升，区分短期节奏调整与需求持续转弱， 同时我
们也不建议仅以生产指数反弹确认修复；二是观察能源价格压力能否缓
解，以及价格指数差值收窄能否转化为中下游利润改善； 三是观察政策需
求能否由装备采购进一步传导至施工和传统原材料行业。截至 10 月 28
日，8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具已全部完成投放；财政部安排的 2500
亿元专项债务结存限额，也优先保障在建项目并支持“六张网”等重点领
域，为后续投资提供资金条件。但资本金投放、配套融资、项目开工到形
成实物工作量可能也会存在时滞。

风险提示：外需订单持续回落；能源价格进一步上涨并挤压中下游利润；
财政金融资金形成实物工作量不及预期；节前需求释放后的消费与补货接
续偏弱。

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价 （或行业
指数） 相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。 其中A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准； 新三板市场以三板成指 （针
对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

Document 017: 2026-10-15_［みずほ経済インサイト］金利上昇による企業の負担増加はいつ本格化するか_eaa924d1

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-10-15_［みずほ経済インサイト］金利上昇による企業の負担増加はいつ本格化するか_eaa924d1.pdf

### Page 1

金利上昇による企業の負担増加は 
いつ本格化するか

○ 日銀の政策金利や、金融市場で決まる国債金利に比べ、企業が実際に負担する金利の上昇はまだ
限定的。企業の新規借入金利や負債残高全体への影響波及に時間がかかることが背景 
○ 金融機関の新規貸出金利は、概ね3カ月かけて政策金利の変化を反映する傾向。特に地域金融機関
で上昇がやや遅く、企業規模により有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが異なる一因に 
○ 借入・社債金利の動きを踏まえると、企業の利払い負担増加は2026～2028年度に本格化し、年間
約3兆円の利益下押し要因に。これまで以上に資本効率を意識した経営が求められる

１．政策金利や国債金利が上昇するも、企業の利払い負担増加はまだ本格化せず 
日本銀行は2026年9月の金融政策決定会合で、 政策金利を1.0％から1.25％へ引き上げた。これは1995
年以来、31年ぶりの高水準である1。また、金融市場で決まる10年国債金利（いわゆる長期金利）は、
2026年9月に節目となる3％を超えた。こちらも1996年以来30年ぶりの高さであり、政策金利や国債金
利はまさに歴史的な転換点を迎えている。 
一方で、 企業が実際に負担する金利の上昇は今のところ限定的だ。 図表1は、 企業の有利子負債利子
率 （＝支払利息等÷有利子負債残高） を指標金利 （政策金利、10年国債金利）と比較したものである。
図表 1 企業の有利子負債利子率と指標金利 図表2 企業の資金繰り判断 DI

（注） 有利子負債利子率は、 支払利息等を有利子負債 （短期 ・
長期借入金＋社債＋受取手形割引残高）の期首・期末
平均値で除した値、対象は金融・保険を除く全産業・
全規模 。凡例中の数値は、マイナス金利政策解除前
（2023年10～12月期）と2026年4～6月期の差

企業統計調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成 
（注） （資金繰りが）「楽である」の回答率から「苦しい」

### Page 2

2

図中の凡例にある数字は、 各金利についてマイナス金利政策解除 （2024年3月19日 ）前 の2023年10～12
月期と、直近の2026年4～6月期を比較した値だ。政策金利が＋0.93％Pt、10年国債金利が＋1.80％Pt
と大きく上昇した一方で、企業の有利子負債利子率は＋0.42％Ptと上昇幅が小さく、企業の利払い負
担の増加はまだ本格化していない。実際、日銀短観における企業の資金繰りに関する調査（図表2）を
みると、「楽である」の回答率から「苦しい」の回答率を差し引いたDIが、2021年以降プラス圏で概
ね横ばいを維持している。企業の実感としても、これまでに実施された複数回の利上げや、金融市場
における国債金利の大幅な上昇は、まだ資金調達環境の悪化につながっていないようだ。 
なぜ、こうしたギャップが生じるのか。主な要因として考えられるのは次の2点である。 
第一の要因は、指標金利と企業の新規調達金利とのタイムラグだ。政策金利などの指標金利が変化
してから、企業が新たに資金を調達する際の金利が変化するまで、少し時間がかかる。特に、企業の
有利子負債の半分強を占める金融機関からの借入において（図表3）、新規調達金利のタイムラグが生
じやすいと考えられる。 
第二の要因は、企業の新規調達金利と有利子負債残高全体の利払い負担とのタイムラグだ。企業が
新たに資金調達する際の金利が上昇しても、それが有利子負債残高全体にすぐ反映されるわけではな
い。 企業の金融機関借入金の4～5割程度が固定金利型であるほか2、 主に大企業が発行する社債の多く
が固定利付債であり、過去の低金利時代に調達した負債がまだ残っているためだ。 
本稿では、 これら2つの要因を踏まえて、政策金利・国債金利などの指標金利上昇を受けた企業の利
払い負担増加が、いつ本格的に現れるかを試算する3。 
２．金融機関からの新規借入金利は、地域金融機関で上昇ペースが遅い傾向 
まず、第一の要因（指標金利から企業の新規調達金利へのタイムラグ）の影響を検証しよう。図表
3に示したように、 企業の有利子負債には金融機関などからの借入金と、 主に大企業が発行する社債が
ある。このうち、社債を新規発行する際の金利は概ね国債金利に連動して決まり、タイムラグがほぼ
図表 3 企業の有利子負債残高の内訳 図表 4 金融機関業態別の新規貸出金利

（注） 2025年度の期初・期末平均。対象は金融・保険を除く
全産業・全規模。有利子負債には受取手形割引残高も
含まれるが、 シェアが小さい （0.1％程度） ため表示し
ていない。

（注） 当該期間中に金融機関が実行した円貸出の金利。企業
向け以外に、個人・政府・地公体向けの貸出も含む

### Page 3

3

無いため、ここでの検証は金融機関などから新規に借り入れる際の金利が対象になる。 
図表4は、金融機関の新規貸出金利（金融機関からみた貸出）を、業態別に示したものだ。都市銀行
では新規貸出金利の上昇ペースが速い一方、第二地方銀行や信用金庫では政策金利の上昇に比べて新
規貸出金利の上昇ペースがやや遅い。こうした地域金融機関における金利上昇の遅れが、指標金利と
企業の新規調達金利のタイムラグに影響していると考えられる。 
金融機関の業態によって新規貸出金利の上昇ペースに差があるのは、貸出の金利形態別シェアが異
なることが一因であろう。大手行では企業向け貸出全体の約6割を占める変動金利型貸出のうち、ほと
んどが「市場金利連動」であるため、金融市場の変化による影響がすぐに反映されやすい。他方で、
地方銀行や信用金庫では変動金利型貸出のうち「短期プライムレート連動」のシェアが高い。短期プ
ライムレートとは、各金融機関が設定する企業向け貸出の最優遇金利である。政策金利の変更などを
反映して決められることが多いが、各金融機関の競争環境などによって反映時期・度合いに差が生じ
る4。これにより、第二地方銀行や信用金庫では都市銀行に比べて新規貸出金利の上昇ペースが遅くな
る傾向があると考えられる5。 
こうした金融機関業態別の違いは、企業サイドにおいて、有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが規模別
に異なる一因になっている可能性がある。足元の金利上昇局面では、都市銀行からの借入が多いと考
えられる大企業（資本金10億円以上）で有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが速く、信用金庫など地域金
融機関からの借入が多いと考えられる小規模企業（同1千万円未満）で上昇ペースが遅い（図表5）。
2010年代の金利低下局面では大企業の有利子負債利子率が小規模企業を下回る傾向にあったが、足元
では完全に逆転している。大企業は社債が有利子負債残高の約3分の1を占めるため、金融機関の新規
貸出金利のみが原因とは言えないものの、金融機関業態別の違いが企業規模別の資金調達環境に影響
を及ぼしている可能性がある点は興味深い。 
では、今後日銀がさらに利上げを進めていくと、金融機関の新規貸出金利はどの程度のペースで、
どこまで上昇していくか。過去の政策金利と新規貸出金利の関係を統計的に分析すると、 政策金利1％

4

Ptの上昇は新規貸出金利を当月に0.31％Pt押し上げ、その後さらに3カ月間かけて当月からの累計で
0.85％Pt押し上げる傾向があることが明らかになった。日銀が今後2027 年半ばにかけて政策金利を
2.0％まで引き上げていくことを前提にすると6、金融機関の新規貸出金利（2026年8月時点で1.78％）
は2027年秋ごろにかけて2.65％程度へ上昇すると試算される （図表6） 。 マイナス金利政策の解除前と
比較すると、2％Pt弱の上昇になる計算だ。 
３．企業の利払い負担増加は今後 2028 年度にかけて本格化。 利益を年間約 3 兆円下押し 
次に、第二の要因（企業の新規調達金利と有利子負債残高全体の利払い負担とのタイムラグ）の影
響を検証しよう。金融機関の新規貸出金利や社債の新規発行金利の上昇が、それぞれの残高全体にど
の程度のペースで反映されるかを確認し、企業の利払い負担がいつ本格化に増加するか試算する。 
金融機関の新規貸出金利と貸出残高全体の金利を比較すると、今回の金利上昇局面では新規貸出金
の上昇に対して貸出金利残高全体の金利上昇がやや遅れている（図表7）。 先に述べたように、金融機
関の貸出の一部が固定金利型であり、新規貸出や借り換えに伴って新規貸出金利の上昇が残高全体へ
波及するのに時間がかかるためだ。 
両者の過去の関係を統計的に分析すると、新規貸出金利の上昇がおよそ5年程度かけて残高全体の金
利を緩やかに押し上げる傾向があるとの結果になった。 図表6で試算したように、 先行きの新規貸出金
利が2027年秋ごろにかけて2.65％程度まで上昇していくと想定すると、貸出残高全体の金利（2026年
8月時点で1.51％ ）は2027年末に2.0％程度、2028年末に2.3％程度と緩やかに上昇し、2030年代前半に
は新規貸出金利と同じ2.65％程度へ収束していく計算になる。 
同様に社債について、 新発債金利と既発債金利を比較したものが図表8だ。 新発債金利は2022年ごろ
から上昇して足元では2％台後半に達しているが、既発債金利はまだ1％強にとどまっており、金融機
関の貸出金利に比べると残高全体への反映に時間がかかりやすいことがうかがえる。なお、日本で新

### Page 5

5

規に発行される社債の平均年限は現在5～6年であり、新発債金利は概ね5 年国債金利に連動して動く
傾向があることから、先に述べたように指標金利→新発債金利のタイムラグはほとんど生じない。 
今後、社債残高が物価上昇にあわせて年間＋2％のペースで増加していくとの前提を置き、 先行きの
償還に伴う借り換えや追加的な新規発行が行われていく場合、既発債金利は2030年代初めにかけて年
間＋0.3％Pt程度の緩やかなペースで上昇していくと試算される（新発債金利は5年国債金利の先行き
予測値に連動すると想定）。7年債や10年債といった年限が長めの社債も一定の規模があることから、
新発債金利の上昇が既発債金利に反映されるまでに時間を要する結果になった。 
最後に、金融機関の貸出金利（企業からみた借入金利7）と社債金利の試算結果を用いて、企業の有
利子負債利回りへの影響を検証する。企業の有利子負債に占める借入と社債の金額シェアを先行き一
定として、 貸出金利と社債金利の変化を有利子負債利子率に反映したものが図表9だ。2023～2025年度
の有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースは年間＋0.1～＋0.2％Pt程度と緩やかだったが、2026～2028年度に
は同＋0.3～＋0.4％Ptに加速すると見込まれる。企業の有利子負債の多くを占める借入金の金利上昇
が、主な押し上げ要因だ。2029年以後は借入金の金利上昇が落ち着く一方、社債金利の上昇継続を受
け、有利子負債利子率は2030年代にかけて緩やかに3％程度の水準まで上昇していくだろう。 
このように、金利上昇による企業の利払い負担増加は今後2028年度にかけて本格化していく見込み
だ。企業全体の有利子負債残高が先行き年間＋2％のペースで拡大していくと仮定すると、 支払利息等
が年間約3兆円増加する計算であり、経常利益（金融・保険を除くベースで2025年度：124.8兆円）に
対し年間2.4％の押し下げ要因に相当する8。 
今後、有利子負債利子率が上昇していく過程では、それに見合った利益率を達成できるかが問われ
るだろう。2025年度時点では投下資本利益率（ROIC、3.70％）の有利子負債利子率（1.45％）に対す
るスプレッドが2％Ptを上回っており、ROICでみた企業の資本効率は外部資金調達コストを十分カバー
できている（図表10）9。しかし、 有利子負債利子率が今後3％程度へ上昇していくと、ROICが現在の水
準のままではスプレッドがコロナ禍初期の2020年度並みに縮小し、企業の利払い負担に対する利益の
図表 9 企業の有利子負債利子率の試算 図表 10 有利子負債利子率と投下資本利益率

残高と社債残高のシェアを一定として、有利子負債利

［参考文献］ 
みずほ総合研究所調査部（2026）「2026年・2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ～転換点は近いのか、 投資
主導経済の行方を探る～」、2026年10月2日

1 現在の政策金利は無担保コール翌日物金利の誘導目標だが、1995 年時点では公定歩合を政策金利と見なして時系列比較してお
り、厳密には政策金利の意味するものが異なる。 
2 日本銀行「金融システムレポート」 （2026年4 月）の図表Ⅲ-1-12 によれば、法人向け貸出のうち、大手行で約40％、地域銀
行（地方銀行、第二地方銀行）や信用金庫で 45～50％程度が固定金利貸出である（2025 年 9 月末時点）。 
3 金利上昇による企業への影響には、利払いの増加によるデメリットだけでなく、利子収入の増加によるメリットもある。本稿
では、そのうちデメリットの面だけに焦点を当てて分析しており、メリットについては稿を改めて論じたい。 
4 日本銀行「金融システムレポート」 （2025年4 月）の図表Ⅵ-1-8 では、地域銀行を対象に、2024 年7 月の政策金利上昇を受け
た短期プライムレートの引き上げタイミング、引き上げ幅、引き上げ後金利の貸出先適用割合について分析が行われている。 
5 図表 4 に示した金融機関業態別の新規貸出金利には、企業向け以外の貸出も含まれる。したがって、企業向け貸出における
「市場金利連動」 、 「短期プライムレート連動」のシェアが業態別に異なることだけでなく、貸出全体に占める住宅ローン貸出
のシェアが業態別に異なることなども、新規貸出金利の上昇ペースの違いに影響していると考えられる。 
6 みずほ総合研究所調査部（2026）の見通しに基づく想定。 
7 金融機関の貸出金利には企業向け以外の貸出も含まれるが、ここでは簡易的に、企業向け貸出金利が貸出全体の金利に連動す
ると仮定して試算した。 
8 本稿では金利上昇による利払いの増加のみを計算対象としているため、利子収入の増加も考慮すれば経常利益への下押し圧力
は緩和すると考えられる。 
9 投下資本利益率（ROIC）と本来比較すべきは、負債だけでなく自己資本のコストも考慮した加重平均資本コスト（WACC）だ
が、本稿では金利上昇の影響を直接評価する観点から、投下資本利益率と有利子負債利子率を比べている。

Document 041: 2026Q3业绩分析：全A盈利增速高位回落，成长延续业绩相对优势

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026Q3业绩分析：全A盈利增速高位回落，成长延续业绩相对优势.pdf
Report source: 华安证券
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 28
Status: ok

### Page 2

2
盈利增速
➢ 2026Q3全A、全A剔金融盈利增速均呈高位回落态势 。其中全 A以及剔金融后全 A在2026Q3单季度归母净利润增速分别为
-14.7%、4.1%，相较2026Q2分别下降40.5、16.1个百分点；创业板、科创板、北证盈利增速也呈大幅回落，增速分别为5.1%、
-58.0%、-10.7%，较2026Q2分别下降35.6、45.9、13.9个百分点。
➢ 2026Q3成长风格盈利增速虽有回落但仍维持业绩相对优势。2026Q3单季度，消费、成长、周期、金融盈利增速分别为-5.2%、
19.5%、-2.3%、-30.7%，成长风格已连续7个季度取得业绩相对优势。
➢ 成长行业中，仅通信盈利增速抬升，其余行业Q3单季盈利增速均较上季度下滑超 20个百分点。其中电子回落较多但仍维持
高增速。通信盈利增速小幅抬升，增速为13.9%，较2026Q2小幅提升了7.0个百分点。
➢ 消费行业盈利增速过半数改善，商贸、医药、纺服盈利增速边际改善相对明显。其中纺织服饰盈利增速转正，美容护理、轻

工制造盈利增速下降较多，美容护理降幅较大且由正转负。
➢ 周期行业中多数下滑，仅农牧、交运较上季度增速改善。钢铁、化工、有色、煤炭边际下滑较多。钢铁、化工、有色、煤炭
2026Q3盈利同比增速分别为 -133.2%、4.2%、56.3%、26.5%，分别下降了116.0、54.6、45.9、40.0个百分点。交运由负转正，
农牧有所好转但仍处于负值状态。
➢ 大金融行业中，非银2026Q3盈利增速大幅下滑，由高增转为负增；银行较上季度小幅改善；房地产盈利增速延续回升。
核心要点
毛利率
➢ 2026Q3除科创板外，其余上市板毛利率均有小幅下滑。剔除金融后的全A、创业板和北证毛利率分别为18.5%、22.1%、
21.6%，较2026Q2变化分别减少了0.3、1.4、0.8个百分点。而科创板毛利率为43.1%，边际提升了11.5个百分点。
➢ 2026Q3季度，成长风格毛利率继续改善，周期、消费小幅下滑。

### Page 3

3
核心要点
毛利率
➢ 毛利率提升显著（3%以上）：电子+4.8%。
➢ 毛利下降显著（-2%以上）：美护-4.3%、钢铁-2.3%、公用事业-2.8%、基础化工-3.1%。
 ROE
➢ 2026Q3全A整体ROE较去年同期下降，销售利润率和总资产周转率是拖累项。科创板ROE较去年同期大幅增长，主要受益于销
售利润率的增长。创业板小幅回落，资产周转率和权益乘数形成拖累。
➢ 风格上，仅成长ROE改善，杜邦三因子均有贡献。消费和金融板块均受销售利润率、总资产周转率的拖累而ROE下滑。
➢ 成长行业中，电子ROE显著提升，主要受益于销售利润率和资产周转率的带动；除通信外，其余成长行业权益乘数均有提升；
除军工外，其余成长行业总资产周转率整体提升。
➢ 消费行业中，仅有商贸、医药、纺织服饰ROE改善，三个行业的销售利润率均形成正向带动。汽车、轻工、家电ROE较去年
同期下滑较多，则是受销售利润率明显拖累。
➢ 周期行业中过半数 ROE走弱，能化板块继续提升。有色、煤炭、石化、化工ROE延续改善态势，其中有色、煤炭、石化主要

是受益销售利润率和权益乘数贡献，化工则主要由资产周转率带动。农牧ROE继续大幅回落，钢铁和公用事业 ROE降幅也偏
大，其中销售利润率大幅下滑仍是主要拖累项。
➢ 金融风格，非银ROE大幅下滑，销售利润率大幅下降和总资产周转率明显下滑形成拖累。银行ROE较去年同期基本持平；房
地产ROE较去年同期有所改善，权益乘数提升对冲了销售利润率和资产周转率下滑的影响。
风险提示：样本数据统计结果与真实情况可能存在偏差、基于个人的数据处理办法也可能存在偏差、业绩表现不等于市场表现等。

1.4 盈利质量：2026Q3全A整体ROE较去年同期下降
➢ 由于单季度ROE环比变化通常存在较强的季节性效应，因此将ROE与去年同期ROE相比来衡量盈利质量的变化。
➢ 2026Q3上市公司整体盈利质量ROE较2025Q3下降较多，销售利润率和总资产周转率是拖累项。A股上市公司2026Q3
的单季度ROE为2.04%，较2025Q3减少0.36个百分点。根据杜邦拆解，销售利润率减少了0.9个百分点，总资产周转率
减少了0.2个百分点，权益乘数提高了0.08个单位点。
2026Q3上市公司整体ROE较去年同期有所下降 销售利润率和资产周转率形成拖累

1.4 盈利质量：科创板ROE较去年同期大幅增长，创业板小幅回落
➢ 2026Q3创业板ROE较去年同期小幅下降，主要是受到了销售利润率的拖累。创业板2026Q3的ROE为2.22%，较2025Q3的
2.28%减少0.06个百分点。根据杜邦拆分，销售利润率下滑了1.0个百分点，而总资产周转率和权益乘数均有抬升。
➢ 2026Q3科创板ROE大幅提升，主要受益于销售利润率的增长，总资产周转率和权益乘数也有增益。科创板2026Q3单季度
ROE为3.68%，较2025Q3的1.00%大幅提升2.68个百分点。按杜邦拆解，科创板销售利润率、总资产周转率、权益乘数分
别增加了10.3个百分点、1.2个百分点、0.14个单位点，销售利润率的提升对科创板ROE形成了显著的带动效应。

➢ 2026Q3成长维持业绩相对优势，截至今年3季度已连续7个季度具备业绩相对优势，产业景气度持续处于扩张区间。
成长风格2026Q3盈利增速较上季度大幅回落22.8个百分点，为4大风格中唯一维持正增的风格。
➢ 除成长风格外，大金融和周期风格盈利增速回落较多，消费风格盈利增速小幅提升。2026Q3周期、大金融风格盈利
同比增速分别为 -2.3%、-30.7%，较上季度分别下降了 20.8、65.4个百分点。消费风格盈利增速较上季度小幅增加 0.4
个百分点，但仍为负值。

Chroma Ate Inc.  | Asia Pacific
Momentum Seen Sustained into
2027
SL T, photonics (transceiver+CPO), and power testing should
continue to drive growth into 2027, with contributions from FT
handlers and burn-in ovens likely emerging as well. Stay OW.
3Q26 miss on GM: See details in Results Summary
Growth momentum seen sustained into 4Q26 and 2027: Management indicated
that the strong business momentum observed in 3Q26 is likely to stay intact going

forward, driven by both power testing and semi & photonics businesses. Normalized
GM remains at ~60% (2026 guidance of ~60% GM implies GM back to ~60% in
4Q26 vs. 57.5% in 3Q26).
• Power testing: 60-70% of revenue is AI-related for 2026, and it is expected
to be sustained into 2027. HVDC testing tools could be the major driver, and
it has already received orders from customers.
• SLT: 9M26 revenue doubled on a YoY basis. SL T demand used to focus on

one major customer, but management indicated that it has added AVGO and
MRVL as new customers. Moreover, demand in 4Q26/1H27 should be mostly
related to inference applications, including CPU/ASIC/LPU.
• Photonics: 9M26 revenue grew by 4x on  a YoY basis. Demand from
transceivers should remain solid and CPO should grow notably in 2027.
Chroma focuses on Insertion 3/4o and shipments for the mass production
line next year  are seen reaching 4-5x vs. that for the pilot line this year.

Moreover, it has added another customer for a pilot run in 9M27 and mass
production in 4Q27. At the industry level, if insertion is excluded from mass
production, Insertion 3 tool demand should be higher. Opportunities will
likely be much larger when CPO penetrates the scale-up network.
• New businesses: FT handlers have been qualified by multiple HPC/CSP
customers and orders have been placed. Burn-in ovens have secured the first
CSP customer, with more HPC customers expected to follow next year.

These should be new addressable markets for Chroma in the coming years.
Stay OW: We believe Chroma should continue to benefit from the AI compute/
networking/power testing opportunities due to its strong customer relationship,
solid execution and outstanding technology expertise. Our PT of NT$2,800 is based
on 40x 2027e P/E, implying a PEG of 0.6 (vs. 1.0 over the past decade).

3Q26 miss on GM:
Off revenue of  NT$17,926mn (+32% QoQ/+180% YoY), GM contracted 3ppt QoQ, to
57.5%, below our estimate at 60.5% and consensus at 62.7%, mainly owing to a less
favorable product mix.
Opex of NT$3,141mn (opex ratio 17.5%) was lower than our estimate at NT$3,797mn (opex
ratio 21.2%) and consensus at NT$3,321mn (opex ratio 21.7%), reflecting operating
leverage.
This led to operating profit of NT$7,167mn (OPM up 1.2ppt QoQ, to 40.0%), 2% higher

than our estimate at NT$7,040mn (OPM 39.3%) and 14% higher than consensus at NT
$6,281mn (OPM 41.0%).

$13.37), 2% short of our estimate at NT$5,790mn (EPS NT$13.71) and 6% short of
consensus at NT$5,317mn (EPS NT$12.59).

A Closer Look
Test instrument and ATS segment looks set to reach another
record high in 2026
Revenue for the segment increased 55% YoY to NT$10,546mn (37% of the mix) in 2025.
We estimate it will rise 118% YoY to NT$24,564mn (40% of the mix) in 2026. The ever-
increasing power consumption of AI clusters should continue to drive demand for higher
spec testing tools. Along with our forecast of a 75% CAGR for global power supply unit

demand in the coming three years (see our report), this should remain a major driver for
Chroma, in our view.

Multiple drivers in the semiconductor business
System-level testing  (SL T) business
SL T should benefit from the capex cycle of NVIDIA's Rubin generation GPU, AMD's MI450
GPU and more ASIC accelerators.  Management pointed out that discrete testing tools are
still preferred  by major customers.  We are also seeing more HPC chip designers adopting

the system level testing process to make sure defects won't be incorporated into the AI
systems. We believe Chroma, as the first mover in this market, should benefit from
increasing chip volume, as well as higher TDP.
Metrology business
The metrology business should continue to benefit from current advanced packaging
architectures from its foundry and OSAT customers. It is confident that the performance
of its tools can win future architecture projects, which our industry checks suggest should

have higher metrology density.

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
Exhibit 4: Chroma –  Offerings for Metrology in Advanced Packaging
Source: Chroma
Chroma's metrology equipment employs white light interferometry technology. The basic
working principle is that white light is emitted on the reference mirror and the sample,
which are placed at the same distance from the light source. The reflected beams from
the reference mirror and sample surface are then received by a light sensor.
Interference occurs when two light beams collide, which could cause  them to either

strengthen or weaken, depending on the phase of the two light beams. Thus, by
measuring the intensity of the combined light beams, the distance between light sensor
and multiple points on the target surface can be calculated through an algorithm. This can
then be used to measure the dimensions of the structure or the alignment of layers.
Exhibit 5: Concept of Light Interference
Source: Keyence
Exhibit 6: Setup of Interferometer
Source: Wikipedia
CPO contribution to start from 2026

Chroma has been qualified for optical engine testing in Insertion 3 and system-level
testing in Insertion 4, and there might be incremental opportunities if supply chain players'
progress falls behind for testing equipment in Insertion 2. Over the longer term, Chroma
also  aspires to participate in the PIC optical/electrical/reliability testing in Insertion 1.

FT handlers to add another revenue stream
In HPC testing, Chroma used to focus mostly on the SL T side, although it did unveil its first
HPC FT handler earlier this year and is now going through the qualification process. We
believe this is a natural extension of its expertise and customer relationships accumulated
in SL Ts. We expect Chroma to gain more traction in FT handlers for AI HPCs in 2027.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 40x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of
~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 40x is justified by the 68% operating profit CAGR we project for

2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected macro conditions
n Stronger semi and photonics and power testing demand
n Margin improvement via better product mix
n Better opex control
Risks to Downside
n Macro slowdown
n Softer-than-expected AI capex
n Slower progress in the advanced packaging capacity expansion
n Weaker consumer electronics and automobile related demand
n Margin contraction arising from lack of revenue scale and increasing opex

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Taiwan Semis: GUC (3443.TW): Mgmt. Call takeaways: Server CPU ramp 
to sustain momentum into 2027; opportunities across applications
31 October 2026 | 12:35AM HKT
We talked to GUC (3443.TW, not covered) management recently. The company’s Sep 
revenues were up 22% MoM (vs. +1% MoM in Aug), reflecting its revenues from CPU 
of a leading US CSP in strong ramp up with more foundry capacity rolling out, and 
would continue to support the growth in 4Q26 and 2027 per the company. The 
positive tone echoes our positive view on Global Servers TAM, which we expect 
leading US cloud service providers (CSPs) in aggregate will grow their capex budgets 
by 84%/ 52%/ 13% YoY in 2026-28E, with our estimates being raised by 4% / 17% / 
22% in 2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 2028E. Revenues in 3Q26 are driven by both 
NRE and turnkey solutions, with turnkey solutions revenues up 44% QoQ on the CPU 
ramp up, and despite turnkey solutions up to ~90% of 3Q26 revenues, the GM 
sustained at 20%+ in 3Q26, thanks to higher-margin NRE milestones. GUC adjusts 
pricing to reflect rising wafer and memory costs, and management sees memory 
impact is manageable on its customers’ projects. On competition, management 
highlighted its position as a core partner of TSMC, with in-house IP for 3D stacking as 
a key differentiator. GUC’s strong turnkey pull-in from its CSP customer’s server CPU 
and its focus on IP for TSMC 3D stacking point to continued demand for TSMC’s 
(2330.TW, Buy) advanced nodes and packaging. The positive tone on ASIC demand 
also echoes our positive view on rising ASIC penetration rate (~60% by 2028E, report 
link), supported by leading CSPs diversifying thier AI chips platforms and reducing 
token per second per costs, driving inferencing / training efficiency.

Company profile: GUC (3443.TW, not covered) is a ASIC design service company 
and a TSMC affiliate, providing NRE design services and turnkey production for

### Page 2

custom chips manufactured at TSMC. Its customers span cloud (CPUs, AI accelerators,
BMCs), automotive and consumer (image sensors, SSD controllers), with 68% of its
products under 7nm.
Key takeaways
1. Mass production as the key profit driver: Management noted that the key to
profitability lies in converting NRE projects into mass production. With the largest
customer’s CPU now in volume production, turnkey has become the main growth driver.
NRE carries higher GM than turnkey, but as it is recognized by milestone, quarterly NRE

margin varies with the mix of projects at different stages.
2. Expanding opportunities across cloud and automotive: GUC continues to open up
new opportunities across applications. In cloud, it works with all major US CSPs on
projects spanning CPUs and GPUs and also supplies other server projects such as BMCs,
while actively pursuing further AI accelerator opportunities. In automotive, it is engaged
with both US and Chinese customers at the NRE stage, two of which already plan for

mass production.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

### Page 1

Auras Technology (3324.TWO)
3Q26 Preview: Deferrals Weigh on 3Q; ASIC Ramp Underpins 
4Q26E/2027E Growth
CITI'S TAKE 
Auras is scheduled to report 3Q26E results in mid-Nov, followed by its 
annual investor meeting in the following weeks. 3Q26 revenue fell short of 
mgmt. ’s guidance of at least +15% QoQ on GB300 demand deferrals and 
Thailand flooding. We expect GPM below our prior forecast on lower scale 
and front-loaded expenses, and forecast 3Q26 net profit of NT$1.24bn, 13% 
below cons. We now model 4Q26E revenue growth of +20% QoQ on 
deferred demand and the ASIC liquid cooling ramp from Nov, and 2027E 
revenue growth of +50% YoY as multiple ASIC projects broaden the growth 
base. We raise our TP to NT$2,000 (20x 2027E EPS). Reiterate Buy.
3Q26 preview — Auras reported 3Q26 revenue of NT$9.8bn (+13% QoQ/+64% YoY), 
below mgmt. ’s earlier guidance. The shortfall mainly reflected customers deferring 
GB300 demand into 4Q26 and the flooding in Thailand. Given lower revenue scale 
and certain expenses recognized ahead of the upcoming ramp, we expect 3Q26E 
GPM to come in at 28.2% (-0.2 ppt QoQ/-1.6 ppts YoY), below our prior forecast of 
30.6%. With the opex ratio largely in line, we forecast 3Q26 net profit of NT$1.24bn 
(+28% QoQ/+27% YoY), and EPS of NT$13.32, 13% below cons.
Multiple ASIC projects to support 4Q26E/2027E — Mgmt. guided for 4Q26E 
revenue growth of +20% QoQ, driven by deferred 3Q26 revenue and the mass 
production of the ASIC project’s liquid cooling version, which we expect from Nov. 
We raise our 4Q26E growth forecast to +20% QoQ (from +16%), with GPM improving 
to 31.1%. While VR cold plate qualification is ongoing, we believe Auras is well 
positioned to win multiple ASIC projects, with a second project likely to contribute 
from 2H27. We now forecast 2027E revenue growth of +45% YoY, with GPM 
expanding to 32.2% on a rising share of customized projects and a better mix.
Implications — We lower our 2026E earnings forecast by 5% on the softer 3Q26. We 
also raise our 2027E/28E earnings forecasts by 13%/20% on higher ASIC liquid 
cooling shipment assumptions and margin expansion. Our revised TP of NT$2,000 
(from NT$1,800) remains based on 20x 2027E EPS. We believe recent concerns over 
the timing of various AI server projects are largely priced in. Given Auras’ structural 
growth in the server business, widening liquid cooling content across GPU and ASIC 
platforms, and an optimizing global manufacturing footprint, we view current 
valuation as attractive and reiterate our Buy rating.

Michael Hung AC

Bull/Bear: Auras Technology (3324.TWO)

### Page 6

Auras Technology (3324.TWO)
02 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Auras Technology
Company description
Auras Technology was founded in 1998 and focused on the design and
manufacturing of thermal modules for NB/PCs. It quickly became one of
Taiwan’s top two NB cooling component suppliers. Early products included
heatsinks, fins, heat pipes, and fans for NBs, PCs, and smartphones. As
device form factor evolved, the company invested in new technologies such

as ultra-thin vapor chamber in the 2000s to address the thermal challenges
of increasingly compact consumer electronics. During the 2010s, Auras
expanded into graphic card and server cooling and was an early adopter of
liquid cooling technology. By 2012, the company had begun developing liquid
cooling component and was among the first Taiwanese firms to venture into
AI server liquid cooling.
Investment strategy
We rate Auras a Buy. Auras is emerging as a key liquid-cooling supplier as

hyperscalers expand deployment. The company has secured qualifications for
cold plates and manifolds, opening access to a wider slate of Nvidia and CSP
projects. Thailand-based production offers non-China capacity at a scale
aligned with hyperscaler sourcing plans, allowing Auras to compete more
effectively for next-generation products.
Valuation
We value Auras using a PE-based approach, reflecting its accelerating
earnings trajectory and rising share in liquid cooling market of AI servers. Our

TP of NT$2,000 is based on 20x 2027E EPS, which we believe is justified
given the company’s expanding server revenue base, deeper engagement
with CSP customers, and broader product portfolio across cold plates, CDMs,
and CDUs.
Risks
This stock is High Risk based upon our quantitative model, but assigning a
High Risk rating is not supported in our view by other qualitative factors such
as rapid RVL progress and Thailand expansion, and so a High Risk rating has

not been applied. Key downside risks to our investment thesis and target
price include: 1) slower adoption of liquid cooling; 2) heightened price
competition; 3) lower-than-expected share in liquid cooling components;
and 4) emerging alternative cooling technologies.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-07

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성전자 (005930)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정  
3Q26E 영업이익 106.1조원, 원화강세 불구 견조한 수준 기록 전망 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익은 106.1조원으로 낮아진 시장 기대치 (104조원)를 소폭 
능가할 전망이다. 해당 분기동안 급격한 원화강세로 인해 실적 측면의 비우호적 환경
이 조성되었지만, 동사는 강건한 판매 정책에 기반해 메모리 판가가 산업 평균을 재
차 능가하며 견조한 실적 개선이 가능했다. 
동사 메모리 사업부의 3Q26 영업이익은 109조원으로 예상되며, 이중 DRAM과 
NAND는 각각 80조원 (OPM 78.5%) 과 29조원 (OPM 68.9%) 을 차지할 전망이다. 
DRAM과 NAND 출하량은 기존 예상치를 소폭 하회하는 +3.4%/+4.2% QoQ을 기
록하겠지만, 판가가 무려 +20.1%/19.0% QoQ 상승하며 실적 개선을 이끌겠다. 전
분기에 이어 ‘DS 특별경영성과급’ 충당금이 반영되며 손익에 일부 영향을 미치겠지
만, 동사 특유의 ‘수익성 극대화’ 판가 정책이 산업 우위 구도를 조성한다. 한편 LSI/
파운드리는 영업손실이 1.5조원을 기록하며 적자가 지속될 전망이다. 가동률 개선에 
기반한 사업 회복이 진행중이지만, 테일러팹 가동과 함께 비용 요인이 지속 출회할 
전망이다. 한편 반도체 외 기타 사업부의 험난한 실적 추이는 지속될 전망이다. SDC 
1.1조원 영업이익을 제외하고, DX (세트) 부문의 경우 MX -1.9조원, DA/VD -7,400
억원으로 영업적자 확대되리라 예상된다. 당사가 지난해 말부터 주장했듯, 세트 사업
은 메모리 등 부품원가 비중이 급격히 상승하는 가운데 향후 ‘판가 인상 → 판매량 
축소’의 ‘내구재화’ 변모 속 적자 최소화 노력만이 불가피할 전망이다.

1Q27 일부 모바일 DRAM 판가 +50% 폭등 예상. 적극적 매수 권고 
AGI를 향한 경쟁적이며 구조적 서버 수요 증가 불구, 스마트폰 중심의 B2C 응용처
는 유일한 판가저항처로 남아있다. 하지만 당사의 채널체크에 따르면, 메모리 업체들
의 지속적인 생산 전환 (LPDDR → 서버 DRAM 및 HBM)에 기반해 주요 고객 향 
모바일 메모리 판가는 무려 1Q27부터 50% 이상 QoQ 상향될 전망이다. 주요 고객
은 내년 20주년 플래그십 스마트폰을 제작하는 상황에서 판가 폭등을 감내해야하는 
상황이다. 최근 메모리 사이클 개선 속도에 대한 막연하며 진부한 우려가 존재하나, 
향후 1~2개월간 메모리 업체들에 대한 2027년 실적 추정치 상향과 함께 ‘성장·저평
가·주주환원 매력’이 동시에 발생할 전망이다. 동사의 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 기
존의 562조원에서 632조원으로 13% 상향조정하며, 시장 눈높이 역시 지속 상향되
리라 예상한다. 한편 배당 중심으로 발표됐던 동사의 주주환원의 경우, 모든 주주에게 
더 실효적인 ‘자사주 매입·소각’ 방안이 병행되는 방향으로 변모하리라 예상된다. 투
자의견 Buy와 적정주가 50만원을 유지한다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 7

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 500,000원

시장과 산업의 시차. 2026 
SMR 첫걸음. 2H27 원전 수
주 확대 본격화

minho.hur@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 85,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
56,700 
유틸리티업종

◼ 목표주가 85,000원(하향). 3분기 실적, 소규모 SMR 수주 등은 긍정적 
◼ 구체적인 미국 원전 사업개발 시작, 2H27 장납기품목 선발주 기대 
◼ 2027년 체코 원전 시공 입찰 재시도, 시공능력의 중요성 강조

목표주가 85,000원(하향). 3분기 실적 양호, 소규모 SMR 수주는 긍정적 
투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 85,000원으로 하향. 이제는 성장 스토리보다
는 실질적인 원전 수주 확대가 필요한 상황. 의미 있는 수준의 원전 수주 확
대는 2H27부터 본격화될 가능성 존재. 다만, 단기적인 원전 수주 모멘텀 부
족 등을 감안해 6개월 목표주가를 현실화했음 
1) 3Q26 연결기준 영업이익 2,612억원(+91%, 이하 YoY), 에너빌리티 부문 
영업이익 1,211억원(+179%) 등 양호한 실적 기록. 이는 에너빌리티의 가스
터빈, 원전 기자재 매출 증가 등에 기인, 2) 10월 29일 NuScale과 1개 모듈 
주기기 공급 계약을 체결했으며, 4Q26 X-energy와 4개 모듈의 일부 장납기 
품목/소재 수주가 예상되는 등 2Q26 ~ 3Q26 TerraPower와의 일부 품목 계
약에 이어 개별 건의 규모는 작지만 다수의 SMR 관련 기자재 수주가 늘어나
는 점은 긍정적. 3) 최근 폴란드 AP1000 3기의 선발주 장납기 품목 확정(폴
란드 공급업체의 대상 생산 품목 확정, 원자로 압력용기 및 증기발생기는 미
확정)되었으며, 미국 원전 프로젝트가 구체화되기 시작. 4) 향후 AP1000향 
공급 기자재 품목 확대 가능성도 존재. 이는 2H27 본격적인 원전 수주금액 
확대로 이어질 것으로 기대

미국 원전은 구체적 사업개발 시작, 2H27 이후 장납기품목 선발주 기대 
2026년 6월 DOE의 ‘원전 공급망 금융지원 사업 발표(AP1000 10기)’, 8월 
TVA의 ‘2026 IRP’에 2040년까지 최대 원전 5.4GW 승인, 9월 한국과 미국 
상무부 간의 원전 투자 8기 MOU 체결 등을 통해 미국 원전 건설 재개 정책 
및 제도 개편이 구체화되고 강화되었음. 미국 원전 시장은 4Q26 ~ 2027년 
구체적인 사업개발 단계를 거쳐(부지 선정, 원전 운영 SPC 설립, 금융계약체
결, COL 신청 등), 2H27 ~ 2028년 장납기품목 선발주, 2028 ~ 2029년 건
설 계약 단계 진입 예상. 동사는 4Q26 미국 및 국내 가스터빈, 국내 공공 해
상풍력 프로젝트용 터빈, 폴란드 AP1000 주기기 등 미국 원전 이외 분야에
서의 수주 확대 예상. 현재 Rolls-Royce 등과도 SMR 주기기 공급 관련 논의 
중이며, NuScale SMR은 2027년 PPA 체결 시, COL 및 FID 이전 SMR 모듈 
11기(4Q26 1기 계약)의 수주가 가능할 것으로 기대

2027년 체코 원전 시공 입찰 재시도, 시공능력의 중요성 강조 
대우건설·두산에너빌리티 컨소시엄의 체코 원전 시공 계약 협상은 종료되었
지만, 2027년 7월 신규 시공사와 계약 예정. 동사는 입찰에 다시 참여할 예
정. 이번 이슈의 핵심 쟁점은 이미 체코 측과 공사비총액(Lump-sum) 계약을 
맺은 한수원이 시공사와 총액계약을 맺기를 원한 것임. 이번 이슈로 체코 두
코바니 원전 EPC 프로젝트 수익성에 대한 우려가 발생할 수 있지만, 실제 건
설 과정에서 추가 비용이 공사비 계약금액(예비비 포함)을 초과할지는 예단하
기 어려움. 하도급 관리, 공기 준수 등 시공능력의 중요성이 강조되는 상황. 
동사는 3년 전부터 체코에 시공 인력 상주하며 시공 환경 자료 확보, 초기 인
허가, 설계 업무 관여해 왔으며, 주기기 공급사로서 기자재 특성을 활용해 설
치까지 수행 가능한 경쟁력 등을 바탕으로 성공적 수주를 목표하고 있음

KOSPI 5937.66

표 2. 웨스팅하우스의 미국 및 글로벌 신규 원전 프로젝트 현황 (초기 사업발굴 단계 제외)  
 프로젝트·사업 기수 발전용량 Cameco 분류 단계 및 비고 
미국 DOE 공급망 금융지원 사업 최대 10기 최대 11.5GW 장납기 품목 조달 금융지원, 7개 유틸리티와 논의 중 
 미 상무부 전략적 협력사업 최대 10기 최대 11.5GW 정부 협력사업(한국은 6기 투자/건설) 
 V.C. Summer 2·3호기 공사 재개 2기 2.3GW 장납기 품목 발주·EPC(LLI/EPC) 
폴란드 Lubiatowo-Kopalino 3기 3.5GW 장납기 품목 발주·EPC(LLI/EPC) 
불가리아 Kozloduy 7·8호기 2기 2.3GW 초기 서비스 계약(ESC) 
우크라이나 Khmelnytskyi 5·6호기 2기 2.3GW 초기 서비스 계약(ESC) 
정부 지원·협력 및 계약 기반 사업 소계  최대 29기 최대 33.4GW FEED·초기 사업발굴 제외 
FEED 사업 네덜란드, 슬로베니아, 핀란드, 스웨덴, 미국 합계 11기 합계 12.7GW 기본설계(FEED) 
총계 FEED 포함 사업군 최대 40기 최대 46.1GW 초기 사업발굴 제외

표 3. DOE의 미국 대형원전 공급망 금융지원 사업 
항목 공식 발표 내용 
조건부 대출 약정 규모 최대 175억 달러·US$17.5 billion 
지원 대상 최대 5개 프로젝트, 프로젝트당 AP1000 2기, 합계 10기 
프로젝트 소유 구조 Westinghouse와 해당 전력회사 또는 에너지기업이 공동 소유 
대출 접근 전 자기자본 요건 프로젝트당 Westinghouse 5억 달러, 상대 전력회사·에너지기업 5억 달러, 합계 10억 달러를 사전에 확약 
조달 방식 Westinghouse가 참여 기업과 함께 장납기품목을 고정가격으로 조달 
조달 시점 자기자본 확약 시점과 기타 조건에 따라 프로젝트별 구매를 순차적으로 진행 
자료: DOE, 대신증권 Research Center

DAISHIN SECURITIES

표 4. DOE의 미국 대형원전 공급망 금융지원 사업의 대상 장납기품목(LLI; Long-Lead Items) 
 품목 설명  대출과 관련된 확인 근거 
원자로압력용기 
(RPV; Reactor Pressure Vessel) 
핵연료와 노심을 수용하고 원자로 냉각재의 압력을 유지하는 
용기. 앞서 설명한 격납용기와 구분되는 원자로 주기기 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
증기발생기 
(SG; Steam Generator) 
원자로에서 나온 열을 전달받아 터빈을 돌릴 증기를 만드는 대
형 열교환기 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
사전제작 구조 모듈 
(PSM; Prefabricated Structural Modules) 
원전 건물의 벽체·바닥 등 구조물을 모듈로 미리 제작하는 방
식의 구성품. AP1000에는 강판과 콘크리트로 구성하는 구조 
모듈이 사용됨 
DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23. 다만, 미국 대
출자료에 개별 모듈 코드까지 열거되지는 않음 
원자로냉각재펌프 
(RCP; Reactor Coolant Pump) 
원자로 냉각재를 순환시켜 원자로에서 발생한 열을 증기발생
기로 전달하는 펌프 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
격납용기 
(CV; Containment Vessel) 
원자로압력용기와 주요 설비를 둘러싸며 방사성 물질의 외부 
방출을 억제하는 대형 강철 밀폐 구조물 
DOE EDF 담당자의 직접 발언에서 확인 — 
2026.10.20 공개 인터뷰 
자료: DOE, 대신증권 Research Center

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.
Exhibit 1: Consumer Spending is Being Supported by Multiple Engines.
A) Jobs growth has stablized. B) Wage growth have helped offset higher prices.

MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Document 345: France’s social demands run up against tightening fiscal constraints

Source PDF: all report pdf/France’s social demands run up against tightening fiscal constraints.pdf
Report source: ING Think

### Page 1

Thursday’s nationwide mobilisation over wages and purchasing
power will add pressure on a French government already
struggling with weak growth, a fragile labour market and an
increasingly unmanageable budget. Its options for responding
to social discontent are narrowing as higher borrowing costs
further weaken the outlook for public debt
Charlotte de Montpellier
Student-led protests have grown into a broader social movement, putting
more pressure on the French government

Pressure mounts for action on purchasing
power
What began as a student-led protest has grown into a broader
social movement, adding to the government’s challenges as it
struggles to get its 2027 budget through parliament.
The mobilisation continued during the school holidays, with
between 320,000 and 500,000 people taking to the streets last
Saturday, fewer than in previous weeks but hardly the pause the

### Page 2

government had hoped for. The next test will come on Thursday, 5
November, when all the main trade unions will mobilise over wages
and purchasing power, with students and public sector workers
expected to join the protests.
Under pressure, the government is considering raising the
minimum wage by more than the automatic increase of around
1% due in January, possibly adding another 0.5 to 0.6 percentage
points. This would be the ﬁrst discretionary increase since 2012. But

employers are strongly opposed, as they are already facing higher
labour costs through planned cuts to social contribution
exemptions. A further rise in the minimum wage could therefore
encourage companies to limit recruitment or reduce their
workforce, at a time when the labour market is already fragile.
Private sector employment has declined for seven consecutive
quarters in annual terms and now stands 0.3% lower than a year
ago, representing a loss of almost 70,000 jobs. A discretionary

increase in the minimum wage would also have implications for the
public sector. It could raise the pay of around 15% of public sector
employees, adding further pressure on the public ﬁnances.
Part of the additional wage cost could also be passed on to
consumers, adding to inﬂationary pressures. Higher energy prices
have already pushed headline inﬂation from 3.1% in September to
an estimated 3.4% in October, while harmonised inﬂation reached
3.8%.

Budget talks are producing a larger deﬁcit,
not a solution
These pressures are making an already diﬃcult budget equation
even harder to solve. After 10 full days of debate and more than
2,300 amendments examined, France’s 2027 budget discussions
appear to be going nowhere, except towards a larger deﬁcit than
the government intended. While the government aims to reduce
the deﬁcit to 5% of GDP in 2027, after an expected 5.4% in 2026,
the measures approved so far have pushed it substantially further

oﬀ course. According to the Finance Ministry, the amendments
adopted during the ﬁrst reading have already worsened the

### Page 3

projected deﬁcit by €23.9bn (total deﬁcit in 2026 will be around
€165bn).
The vote on the revenue part has been postponed again, as MPs
continue to adopt measures pointing in all directions. Yet the
exercise increasingly makes little sense: the fragmented National
Assembly is unlikely to approve the budget, leaving the
government with two possible escape routes. It could use Article
49.3 to pass the bill without a vote, but would ﬁrst need to
negotiate with parts of the opposition to ensure that a subsequent

no-conﬁdence motion fails. For now, there are few signs of such
talks. If the motion passed, the government would fall and the
budget would be rejected. Alternatively, once the constitutional 70-
day deadline expires on 18 December, the government could
implement its original budget by ordinance. A two-stage solution,
with the ordinance followed by a corrective bill, is reportedly
gaining support among MPs, including some Socialists. The
corrective bill could accommodate selected social promises.

Weak growth makes the ﬁscal targets
unrealistic
The problem is that the government’s initial budget will no longer
be suﬃcient to bring the deﬁcit down to 5% of GDP. It was based on
GDP growth of 1% in 2027, an assumption that now looks clearly
too optimistic. The French economy contracted by 0.1% in the third
quarter, after stagnating in the second and shrinking by 0.2% in the
ﬁrst, leaving GDP marginally lower than a year earlier and France
hovering close to recession.

The details of the third-quarter ﬁgure were also weak: investment
fell by 0.6% and net trade subtracted 0.6 percentage points from
growth as exports dropped sharply, while inventories provided a
temporary oﬀset. The only real positive surprise came from
household consumption, which rose by 0.4%. But there is little
reason to expect this momentum to last. Consumer conﬁdence fell
by ﬁve points to 80 in October, its lowest level since June 2013 and
well below its long-term average of 100. Households’ assessment

of whether this is a good time to make major purchases dropped

### Page 4

sharply, while fears of unemployment increased. Most strikingly,
their assessment of living standards fell to its lowest level since the
survey began in October 1972. This points to much weaker
consumption in the coming months.
The outlook for investment is equally weak. Financing conditions
are deteriorating particularly sharply in France: according to the
latest ECB Bank Lending Survey, a net 27% of French banks
tightened credit standards for businesses in the third quarter,

compared with around 8% in Germany, no tightening in Spain and
an easing in Italy. Together with weak demand, higher interest
rates and uncertainty over taxes and public spending, this is likely
to encourage companies and households to postpone projects.
Combined with political uncertainty and social tensions, growth is
likely to be very weak in the fourth quarter as well. All in all, we now
expect GDP growth of just 0.3% this year and 0.5% in 2027, with

risks still tilted to the downside. Under these assumptions,
implementing the budget unchanged would leave the deﬁcit at
around 5.5% of GDP. Reaching the government’s 5% target would
require roughly €12bn in additional savings, on top of the €43bn
eﬀort already included in the budget, which looks highly unlikely in
the current political environment. And that is before including the
almost €500mn cost of the measures promised to high-school and
university students.

Debt is unlikely to stabilise anytime soon
All this leaves France’s debt dynamics looking increasingly
uncomfortable. The government had expected public debt to reach
121.7% of GDP in 2027, already a level at which a 5% deﬁcit would
be insuﬃcient to stabilise the debt ratio. With growth now likely to
be weaker and the deﬁcit higher than assumed in the budget, debt
will probably rise even further, and there is little prospect of
stabilisation anytime soon.

The sharp rise in borrowing costs only makes the arithmetic worse.
The French 10-year yield is currently around 4.9%, more than
155bp above its German equivalent, compared with a spread of
around 70bp at the end of 2025. Admittedly, the increase in market

### Page 5

yields feeds only gradually into the eﬀective cost of the debt as
existing bonds mature: the implicit interest rate on French public
debt is still around 2.2% in 2026. But this also means that a
substantial part of the rise in interest costs has yet to come
through. Fitch estimates that France would need to reduce its
primary deﬁcit from around 2.9% of GDP this year to just 0.4% to
stabilise the debt ratio. The adjustment required would become
considerably larger if the eﬀective interest rate on the debt

gradually converged towards current market yields.
And the 2027 presidential election is unlikely to provide an easy
reset. A new president could again ﬁnd her or himself without a
parliamentary majority, making the large and sustained ﬁscal
adjustment required increasingly diﬃcult to deliver. These concerns
prompted Fitch to take the unusual step on 30 October of bringing
forward its assessment and revising the outlook on France’s A+
rating from stable to negative, only a week after Moody’s

unexpectedly kept its Aa3 rating unchanged, albeit with a negative
outlook. Fitch explicitly pointed to weaker growth, higher borrowing
costs and a wider deﬁcit, together with political fragmentation and
social tensions that make sustained ﬁscal consolidation harder to
achieve. It expects French public debt to reach around 128% of GDP
by 2030, considerably above the roughly 123% projected by the
IMF.
All in all, tomorrow’s demonstrations will once again underline the

population’s frustration over purchasing power. But without
stronger economic growth to lift household incomes while
simultaneously easing the pressure on the public ﬁnances, the
government has very limited room to oﬀer measures large enough
to make a meaningful diﬀerence without making an already
worrying ﬁscal trajectory even worse.

Document 351: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ Government Bond Yields Ease, Gold Falls

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ Government Bond Yields Ease, Gold Falls.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-01
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Government Bond Yields Ease, Gold Falls
Date:    43 minutes ago
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Government Bond Yields Ease, Gold Falls

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL:
Government Bond Yields Ease,
Gold Falls
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 1 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Gaël Gaborel - OrbisTerrae / Unsplash
Investors sought safety in government bonds last week as the AI selloff
rattled stock markets worldwide. Yields eased across most of the major
bond markets. The stock market selloff has been hardest on the markets
that soared the most this year. October is ending with the US well ahead
of the rest of the world. That validates our recent shift to market weight on
the Go Global portfolio strategy. Here’s more:

I. Stock Market Performance
The Stay Home vs Go Global ratios have rebounded sharply since mid-
year, driven by US outperformance (chart). They remain below their early
2025 peaks but are heading back up.
The US MSCI’s share of the All Country World MSCI’s market cap has
risen back to 65.3% (chart). Its share of forward earnings is 54.3%. That
11-point gap is the widest since January 2025. US stocks again are
pricing in far more than their share of global earnings.

### Page 3

October’s biggest losers were this year’s AI winners. South Korea finished
the month at the bottom of the country ETF rankings, down 16.4% in
dollars (chart). Taiwan fell 10.4%. Switzerland topped the board with an
8.8% gain, followed by Mexico (4.8%). The US slipped 0.9%, while the All
Country World ex-US ETF fell 2.9%.
II. Stock Markets Earnings & Valuation

### Page 4

Overseas forward earnings momentum is the strongest in over 15 years.
Forward earnings for the All Country World ex-US MSCI is up 41.7% y/y,
topping its 2021 post-pandemic peak (chart). Forward revenues is up
11.6% y/y. Earnings outpacing revenues by that much implies forward
profit margins are widening significantly.
Japan leads the major markets in forward earnings growth since early
2009 (chart). Its forward earnings has risen 720.7% in yen. The US is up

524.6%. EM forward earnings has surged this year and is now up 224.7%
over the same period. The EMU and the UK have barely doubled.
Valuation multiples are falling even as earnings soar (chart). The US
MSCI’s forward P/E has dropped to 19.1 from 23.7 a year ago. Japan’s is

### Page 5

14.7, and the EMU’s is 13.9. The UK trades at 11.9. EM trades at 8.9, its
lowest since the 2008 financial crisis.
III. Fixed Income
Government bond yields eased last week in most major markets in
response to the AI selloff (chart). The US 10-year Treasury yield has fallen
to 4.91% from 5.00% a week earlier. France’s 10-year government bond
yield has eased to 4.98%. The UK’s 5.30% and Australia’s 5.14% remain
the highest in the group. Japan’s yield has dropped to 2.76%.

IV. Gold
We've been bullish on the gold price since it jumped to a new record high
above $2,000 per ounce in early 2024. We reckoned that buying by the

### Page 6

central banks of countries that feared the US and Europe might freeze
their forex reserves, as happened to Russia after it invaded Ukraine,
which would be bullish for gold. That was true until the war started in the
Middle East on February 28, 2026 (chart).
We aren't sure why gold hasn't bounced off of its key support levels.
Instead, it dropped back below $4,000 last week. The next support is
around $3,400. Rising interest rates may explain why gold has been so

weak if central bank buying is being swamped by selling from private
investors.
The People's Bank of China is still buying gold (chart). Chinese investors
have lost lots of money in their property and stock markets. Now, they
may be bailing out of gold.

### Page 7

Is there still a case for gold to reach a price of $10,000 per ounce by the
end of the decade? Yes, but the case is getting weaker. If the S&P 500
reaches 10,000 by the end of the decade, investors might rebalance their
portfolios by allocating more to gold (chart).
V. Focus On China & Brazil
China’s MSCI stock price index has fallen to 66.4 in dollars, down from
92.0 at its 2025 high (chart). The property bust and deflation continue to
weigh on the market. So does the rapidly aging population.

Chinese tech has missed the AI boom entirely (chart). The China
Technology ETF (CQQQ) has fallen to 37.78, 65.2% below its February
2021 record high. The Nasdaq-100 ETF (QQQ) is 9.5% below its record
high. We continue to favor EM ex-China over China.

### Page 8

In Brazil, President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva won the October 25 runoff
with nearly 52% of valid votes after trailing Senator Flávio Bolsonaro in the
first round. Investors had positioned for a Bolsonaro win, and Bolsonaro
says he will challenge the result in court. The Brazil MSCI stock price
index fell to 3,508.86 in local currency, giving back most of its post-first-
round gains (chart). It is now up just 1.0% ytd in local currency and 2.2%
in dollars.

ITEM DEFINITIONS AND METHODOLOGY
3© 2026 LSEG.  All marks herein are used under licence. TRPR_82222_875
General:
All aggregates are bottom-up aggregates calculated from analysts' consensus forecasts unless otherwise noted. Bottom-up
aggregates are weighted calculations using the I/B/E/S data from LSEG share class monitored or the official index weight of
the index-provider. Top-down forecasts are compilations of strategists' estimates. I/B/E/S universe calculations are based on

all companies within a given market monitored by LSEG.
Benchmark index calculations, including those for official local market indices, the S&P 500 Index, and the FTSE World Equity
Indices are calculated using official index constituents and weightings. All index composites are representative of current
index constituents. Index constituents and weightings are updated monthly during options expiration (the Thursday before the
third Friday of every month) for every index except the S&P 500, the constituents of which are current as of the date of

calculation.
PEIGBY refers to the Price Earnings Inverse Government Bond Yield ratio, which measures equity valuation with respect to
the inverse-yield on long-term government bonds (now known as the 'Fed' model). All PEIGBY ratios are I/B/E/S universe-
specific except for the US ratio, where it is based on the S&P 500 Index.
Methodology:
For US and selected Canadian aggregates, calendar-year aggregation is performed using the sum of four quarters' estimates

and a quarterly pro-ration is applied to non-December year end companies' quarterly EPS estimates. Outside of the US and
Canada, all company-level data are calendarized prior to aggregation using the Compustat method of calendarization.
Aggregation is performed using the annual consensus EPS estimate and a May roll-back is applied to December year-end
markets. Aggregates for Japan, where most companies report in March, use an August rollback and a November rollback is

used for Australia and New Zealand, where the fiscal year-end for most companies is in June.
All 12-month-forward data for ex-US markets are calculated using a pro-rata weighting to annual EPS forecasts. The
weightings applied are a daily calibration out of 365 days relative to each constituent's individual fiscal year-end and the
current date.
All non-surprise data, including aggregate forecast earnings, forecast growth, and forward-looking valuation calculations are

weighted according to the I/B/E/S share class monitored for I/B/E/S universes or the weightings assigned to each constituent
in a given index. Aggregated earnings calculations are the equivalent of net profit aggregates, as they are derived from the
EPS forecasts aggregated to the net income level by the appropriate share weighting.
Earnings Aggregation:
For 12-month-forward earnings, the 12-month-forward EPS is used in place of the calendarized EPS in the formulas below.

I/B/E/S universe EPS Aggregate = Index EPS Aggregate =
Growth Calculations:
All growth calculations are the weighted year over year growth in earnings for the calendarized fiscal year period. The
formulas below use index shares for index growth calculations.
Calendarized Earnings Growth = 12-month-forward Growth =
Valuation:
All valuation calculations are the weighted-average price-earnings ratio for the fiscal period in question. The formulas below

use index shares for index P/E calculations.
Calendarized P/E Aggregate = 12-month-forward P/E =
Revisions Momentum:
Unless otherwise indicated, analysts' revisions momentum is a simple aggregation of the total number of estimates raised
divided by the total number of estimates lowered. Thus, any revisions ratio less than 1 indicates negative revisions
momentum while a ratio greater than 1 indicates positive revisions momentum, with 1 marking parity.

The Global Aggregates from LSEG product contains annual aggregates for universes included in this publication, including
supplemental aggregate data of over 100 items as well as history dating back to 1985 for North America and to 1987 for other
markets. To learn more about this product, contact your LSEG sales representative.
FTSE and the FTSE World Equity Indices are owned by FTSE International Limited.
